Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I think… I understand it’s only me hearing it. I mean, I’ve never had anyone else react like they heard him too.

But in the moment, it’s so clear—like Garrett is standing right behind me—that I guess there’s a split second where I feel like, “How could no one else hear that?” Then I remind myself he’s dead and this is something happening to me. I’m not saying other people can hear it… I don’t think that. Is that what you mean?

Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you thought someone else might actually be able to hear Garrett’s voice too?\n\nPatient: Not in a real, settled way, no. I don’t think I’ve ever truly believed someone else could hear him too.\n\nIt’s more that when it happens, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room—that for a second I almost expect the world to react to it. Like, “How is this only me?” But then I remember… Garrett’s dead, and I know it has to be something I’m experiencing.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that Garrett has actually returned in a way that others should be able to notice, like he’s physically back among people?\n\nPatient: No… not like that. I don’t think I’ve believed Garrett is physically back, like other people should be seeing him walking around or noticing him.\n\nIt’s more… the voice is the problem. That’s why I came in, really. I keep hearing him so clearly—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room—and even though part of me knows he’s dead, when it’s happening it feels completely real. But I’m not telling myself he’s back among people, if that distinction makes sense.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had any firm beliefs or thoughts that Garrett has returned in some form—like through a spiritual presence, reincarnation, or some other way—that you feel strongly is true, even briefly?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t think I’ve believed Garrett is physically back, like other people should be seeing him walking around or noticing him.\n\nIt’s more… the voice is the problem. That’s why I came in, really. I keep hearing him so clearly—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room—and even though part of me knows he’s dead, when it’s happening it feels completely real. But I’m not telling myself he’s back among people, if that distinction makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any firm beliefs or thoughts that Garrett has returned in some form—like through a spiritual presence, reincarnation, or some other way—that you feel strongly is true, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not as a firm belief.\n\nI mean, I’ve wondered things in that loose, grief kind of way—like, “Is there some part of him still around?”—but I don’t think I’ve felt strongly that he’s reincarnated or spiritually present in a way that’s definitely true. I’m trying not to make it sound more normal than it is, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had a fixed belief like that.\n\nIs that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any other experiences where you see, feel, or smell something that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I’m trying not to make it sound more normal than it is, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had a fixed belief like that.\n\nIs that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other experiences where you see, feel, or smell something that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the hearing part. I… I don’t know if I’d say “completely certain” in a belief sense. I’m not trying to dodge the question.

When it happens, though—when I hear Garrett’s voice, like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—it feels completely real in that moment. Like my body reacts as if he’s there. But another part of me knows he’s dead, so afterward I can say, “No, he hasn’t come back.”

up writing whole outlines for new treatment programs, emailing colleagues, offering to mentor extra people, and starting canvases all at once even though none of it was realistic.\n- key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: People kept telling me to slow down because I was talking over them, jumping from clinical ideas to Garrett to theater plans, and I felt this pressure in my chest to keep going before the words disappeared.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or funeral before I can catch any of it.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I can’t I’m trying not to make it sound more normal than it is, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had a fixed belief like that.\n\nIs that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other experiences where you see, feel, or smell something that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the hearing part. I don’t think I’ve had much with seeing or smelling things, at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nWhat’s been frightening is Garrett’s voice—hearing it as clearly as if he’s standing right behind me, like on the trail or in my painting room. And I know, rationally, that he’s dead… but in that moment it feels completely real. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even briefly, felt completely certain that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back in some way—despite knowing he’s passed away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say “completely certain” in a belief sense. I know Garrett is dead, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve lost touch with that.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it doesn’t feel like a trick. Then afterward I can sort of step back and question it. I do know he’s dead.\n\nBut when I hear his voice, it’s so clear—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in that moment it doesn’t feel like just a vivid feeling. It feels real. Then afterward I can tell myself it must be my mind doing something, but while it’s happening… I’m not so sure.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, do you ever think that others might be able to hear it too, or do you understand that it’s something only you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I think… I understand it’s only me hearing it. I’m not saying other people can hear it… I don’t think that. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having this experience that’s gotten hard to ignore. I keep hearing Garrett’s voice—like, very clearly—as if he’s right behind me, sometimes when I’m out on the trail or in my painting room.\n\nI know that probably sounds… concerning. And part of me does know he’s dead, I do. But in the moment it doesn’t feel like a memory or my imagination. It feels completely real. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever experience things that aren’t there besides hearing Garrett’s voice, like seeing things, feeling something that isn’t touching you, or smelling things that others don’t notice?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, nothing I’d want to overstate. It’s mainly Garrett’s voice. That’s the thing that feels… real. Like he’s right behind me when I’m on the trail, or in my painting room, and I’ll turn like I expect to see him.\n\nI know—at least part of me knows—he’s dead. But when it happens, it doesn’t feel like I’m remembering him. It feels like he’s there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice and feel like he’s there, do you believe he has actually returned in some way, or do you think it’s just your mind playing tricks on you?\n\nPatient: I… I try to tell myself it’s my mind playing tricks on me. I know Garrett is dead, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve lost touch with that.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it doesn’t feel like a trick. It feels completely real—like his voice is right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room. In that moment, part of me feels like he’s actually there. Then afterward I can sort of step back and question it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like Garrett is actually there, do you ever believe he has truly come back, or do you think it's just a vivid illusion or feeling?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I believe he’s truly come back… at least, I don’t want to say that, because I know how it sounds. I do know he’s dead.\n\nBut when I hear his voice, it’s so clear—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in that moment it doesn’t feel like just a vivid feeling. It feels real. Then afterward I can tell myself it must be my mind doing something, but while it’s happening… I’m not so sure.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, do you ever think that others might be able to hear it too, or do you understand that it’s something only you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I think… I understand it’s only me hearing it. I mean, I’ve never had anyone else react like they heard him too.\n\nBut in the moment, it’s so clear—like Garrett is standing right behind me—that I guess there’s a split second where I feel like, “How could no one else hear that?” Then I remind myself he’s dead and this is something happening to me. I’m not saying other people can hear it… I don’t think that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you thought someone else might actually be able to hear Garrett’s voice too?\n\nPatient: Not in a real, settled way, no. I don’t think I’ve ever truly believed someone else could hear him too.\n\nIt’s more that when it happens, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room—that for a second I almost expect the world to react to it. Like, “How is this only me?” But then I remember… Garrett’s dead, and I know it has to be something I’m experiencing.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that Garrett has actually returned in a way that others should be able to notice, like he’s physically back among people?\n\nPatient: No… not like that. I don’t think I’ve believed Garrett is physically back, like other people should be seeing him walking around or noticing him.\n\nIt’s more… the voice is the problem. That’s why I came in, really. I keep hearing him so clearly—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room—and even though part of me knows he’s dead, when it’s happening it feels completely real. But I’m not telling myself he’s back among people, if that distinction makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any firm beliefs or thoughts that Garrett has returned in some form—like through a spiritual presence, reincarnation, or some other way—that you feel strongly is true, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not as a firm belief.\n\nI mean, I’ve wondered things in that loose, grief kind of way—like, “Is there some part of him still around?”—but I don’t think I’ve felt strongly that he’s reincarnated or spiritually present in a way that’s definitely true. I’m trying not to make it sound more normal than it is, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had a fixed belief like that.\n\nIs that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other experiences where you see, feel, or smell something that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the hearing part. I don’t think I’ve had much with seeing or smelling things, at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nWhat’s been frightening is Garrett’s voice—hearing it as clearly as if he’s standing right behind me, like on the trail or in my painting room. And I know, rationally, that he’s dead… but in that moment it feels completely real. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even briefly, felt completely certain that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back in some way—despite knowing he’s passed away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say “completely certain” in a belief sense. I’m not trying to dodge the question.\n\nWhen it happens, though—when I hear Garrett’s voice, like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—it feels completely real in that moment. Like my body reacts as if he’s there. But another part of me knows he’s dead, so afterward I can say, “No, he hasn’t come back.”\n\nDoes that make sense? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever experience things that aren’t there besides hearing Garrett’s voice, like seeing things, feeling something that isn’t touching you, or smelling things that others don’t notice?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, nothing I’d want to overstate. It’s mainly Garrett’s voice. That’s the thing that feels… real. Like he’s right behind me when I’m on the trail, or in my painting room, and I’ll turn like I expect to see him.\n\nI know—at least part of me knows—he’s dead. But when it happens, it doesn’t feel like I’m remembering him. It feels like he’s there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice and feel like he’s there, do you believe he has actually returned in some way, or do you think it’s just your mind playing tricks on you?\n\nPatient: I… I try to tell myself it’s my mind playing tricks on me. I know Garrett is dead, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve lost touch with that.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it doesn’t feel like a trick. It feels completely real—like his voice is right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room. In that moment, part of me feels like he’s actually there. Then afterward I can sort of step back and question it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like Garrett is actually there, do you ever believe he has truly come back, or do you think it's just a vivid illusion or feeling?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I believe he’s truly come back… at least, I don’t want to say that, because I know how it sounds. I do know he’s dead.\n\nBut when I hear his voice, it’s so clear—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in that moment it doesn’t feel like just a vivid feeling. It feels real. Then afterward I can tell myself it must be my mind doing something, but while it’s happening… I’m not so sure.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, do you ever think that others might be able to hear it too, or do you understand that it’s something only you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I think… I understand it’s only me hearing it. I mean, I’ve never had anyone else react like they heard him too.\n\nBut in the moment, it’s so clear—like Garrett is standing right behind me—that I guess there’s a split second where I feel like, “How could no one else hear that?” Then I remind myself he’s dead and this is something happening to me. I’m not saying other people can hear it… I don’t think that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you thought someone else might actually be able to hear Garrett’s voice too?\n\nPatient: Not in a real, settled way, no. I don’t think I’ve ever truly believed someone else could hear him too.\n\nIt’s more that when it happens, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room—that for a second I almost expect the world to react to it. Like, “How is this only me?” But then I remember… Garrett’s dead, and I know it has to be something I’m experiencing.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that Garrett has actually returned in a way that others should be able to notice, like he’s physically back among people?\n\nPatient: No… not like that. I don’t think I’ve believed Garrett is physically back, like other people should be seeing him walking around or noticing him.\n\nIt’s more… the voice is the problem. That’s why I came in, really. I keep hearing him so clearly—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room—and even though part of me knows he’s dead, when it’s happening it feels completely real. But I’m not telling myself he’s back among people, if that distinction makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any firm beliefs or thoughts that Garrett has returned in some form—like through a spiritual presence, reincarnation, or some other way—that you feel strongly is true, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not as a firm belief.\n\nI mean, I’ve wondered things in that loose, grief kind of way—like, “Is there some part of him still around?”—but I don’t think I’ve felt strongly that he’s reincarnated or spiritually present in a way that’s definitely true. I’m trying not to make it sound more normal than it is, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had a fixed belief like that.\n\nIs that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other experiences where you see, feel, or smell something that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the hearing part. I don’t think I’ve had much with seeing or smelling things, at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nWhat’s been frightening is Garrett’s voice—hearing it as clearly as if he’s standing right behind me, like on the trail or in my painting room. And I know, rationally, that he’s dead… but in that moment it feels completely real. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even briefly, felt completely certain that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back in some way—despite knowing he’s passed away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say “completely certain” in a belief sense. I’m not trying to dodge the question.\n\nWhen it happens, though—when I hear Garrett’s voice, like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—it feels completely real in that moment. Like my body reacts as if he’s there. But another part of me knows he’s dead, so afterward I can say, “No, he hasn’t come back.”\n\nDoes that make sense? It’s not that I believe he’s returned exactly… it’s that hearing him feels that real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a short time, believed with complete certainty that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back—despite knowing he has passed away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not with complete certainty, no.\n\nI can see why you’re asking, because in the moment it can feel… very convincing, physically, like I’m reacting before I’ve had a chance to think. But I don’t believe he’s actually come back. At least, I don’t think I’ve crossed into being certain of that.\n\nI’m trying to answer that accurately and not just give the “right” answer. But my best answer is no—not complete certainty.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you were absolutely sure—without any doubt—that Garrett had come back, even if only for a few seconds?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had that absolute “no doubt” feeling.\n\nI’m sorry, I know you’re trying to get at something specific. The closest I can say is that it can feel very real for a moment, like emotionally or physically real, but I don’t think I’ve ever been completely sure he’d actually come back. I don’t think I can honestly say yes to “unshakable.”\n\nIt’s more like… it can hit me hard enough that I pause, or I turn around, or I feel like he’s near me. But there’s still some part of me that knows that can’t be true. I might not feel calm about it in the moment, but I don’t think I’ve believed it with no doubt.\n\nI hope that’s answering it the way you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know Garrett is dead, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve lost touch with that.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it doesn’t feel like a trick. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like Garrett is actually there, do you ever believe he has truly come back, or do you think it's just a vivid illusion or feeling?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I believe he’s truly come back… at least, I don’t want to say that, because I know how it sounds. I do know he’s dead.\n\nBut when I hear his voice, it’s so clear—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in that moment it doesn’t feel like just a vivid feeling. It feels real. Then afterward I can tell myself it must be my mind doing something, but while it’s happening… I’m not so sure.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, do you ever think that others might be able to hear it too, or do you understand that it’s something only you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I think… I understand it’s only me hearing it. I mean, I’ve never had anyone else react like they heard him too.\n\nBut in the moment, it’s so clear—like Garrett is standing right behind me—that I guess there’s a split second where I feel like, “How could no one else hear that?” Then I remind myself he’s dead and this is something happening to me. I’m not saying other people can hear it… I don’t think that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you thought someone else might actually be able to hear Garrett’s voice too?\n\nPatient: Not in a real, settled way, no. I don’t think I’ve ever truly believed someone else could hear him too.\n\nIt’s more that when it happens, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room—that for a second I almost expect the world to react to it. Like, “How is this only me?” But then I remember… Garrett’s dead, and I know it has to be something I’m experiencing.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that Garrett has actually returned in a way that others should be able to notice, like he’s physically back among people?\n\nPatient: No… not like that. I don’t think I’ve believed Garrett is physically back, like other people should be seeing him walking around or noticing him.\n\nIt’s more… the voice is the problem. That’s why I came in, really. I keep hearing him so clearly—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room—and even though part of me knows he’s dead, when it’s happening it feels completely real. But I’m not telling myself he’s back among people, if that distinction makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any firm beliefs or thoughts that Garrett has returned in some form—like through a spiritual presence, reincarnation, or some other way—that you feel strongly is true, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not as a firm belief.\n\nI mean, I’ve wondered things in that loose, grief kind of way—like, “Is there some part of him still around?”—but I don’t think I’ve felt strongly that he’s reincarnated or spiritually present in a way that’s definitely true. I’m trying not to make it sound more normal than it is, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had a fixed belief like that.\n\nIs that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other experiences where you see, feel, or smell something that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the hearing part. I don’t think I’ve had much with seeing or smelling things, at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nWhat’s been frightening is Garrett’s voice—hearing it as clearly as if he’s standing right behind me, like on the trail or in my painting room. And I know, rationally, that he’s dead… but in that moment it feels completely real. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even briefly, felt completely certain that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back in some way—despite knowing he’s passed away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say “completely certain” in a belief sense. I’m not trying to dodge the question.\n\nWhen it happens, though—when I hear Garrett’s voice, like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—it feels completely real in that moment. Like my body reacts as if he’s there. But another part of me knows he’s dead, so afterward I can say, “No, he hasn’t come back.”\n\nDoes that make sense? It’s not that I believe he’s returned exactly… it’s that hearing him feels that real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a short time, believed with complete certainty that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back—despite knowing he has passed away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not with complete certainty, no.\n\nI can see why you’re asking, because in the moment it can feel… very convincing, physically, like I’m reacting before I’ve had a chance to think. But I don’t believe he’s actually come back. At least, I don’t think I’ve crossed into being certain of that.\n\nI’m trying to answer that accurately and not just give the “right” answer. The closest I can say is that it can feel very real for a moment, like emotionally or physically real, but I don’t think I’ve ever been completely sure he’d actually come back. At least not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a brief moment, believed in a way that felt unshakable at the time—no matter how strange it might seem—that Garrett had somehow truly returned to you, even though you knew he was gone?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking. I don’t think I can honestly say yes to “unshakable.”\n\nIt’s more like… it can hit me hard enough that I pause, or I turn around, or I feel like he’s near me. But there’s still some part of me that knows that can’t be true. I might not feel calm about it in the moment, but I don’t think I’ve believed it with no doubt.\n\nI hope that’s answering it the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, even for a short time, that Garrett's presence was real in any way beyond just hearing his voice—like thinking he was actually with you in some physical or tangible form?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so… at least not that I can clearly point to.

I’ve had moments where it felt confusing or emotionally intense, and afterward I sort of had to steady myself and remind myself what’s real. But believing he had actually returned, and then later realizing that belief was false… I don’t think I’ve had that in a definite way.

I keep hearing Garrett’s voice—like, very clearly—as if he’s right behind me, sometimes when I’m out on the trail or in my painting room.\n\nI know that probably sounds… concerning. And part of me does know he’s dead, I do. But in the moment it doesn’t feel like a memory or my imagination. It feels completely real. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever experience things that aren’t there besides hearing Garrett’s voice, like seeing things, feeling something that isn’t touching you, or smelling things that others don’t notice?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, nothing I’d want to overstate. It’s mainly Garrett’s voice. That’s the thing that feels… real. Like he’s right behind me when I’m on the trail, or in my painting room, and I’ll turn like I expect to see him.\n\nI know—at least part of me knows—he’s dead. But when it happens, it doesn’t feel like I’m remembering him. It feels like he’s there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice and feel like he’s there, do you believe he has actually returned in some way, or do you think it’s just your mind playing tricks on you?\n\nPatient: I… I try to tell myself it’s my mind playing tricks on me. I know Garrett is dead, and I don’t want to sound like I’ve lost touch with that.\n\nBut when it’s happening, it doesn’t feel like a trick. It feels completely real—like his voice is right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room. In that moment, part of me feels like he’s actually there. Then afterward I can sort of step back and question it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like Garrett is actually there, do you ever believe he has truly come back, or do you think it's just a vivid illusion or feeling?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I believe he’s truly come back… at least, I don’t want to say that, because I know how it sounds. I do know he’s dead.\n\nBut when I hear his voice, it’s so clear—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in that moment it doesn’t feel like just a vivid feeling. It feels real. Then afterward I can tell myself it must be my mind doing something, but while it’s happening… I’m not so sure.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, do you ever think that others might be able to hear it too, or do you understand that it’s something only you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I think… I understand it’s only me hearing it. I mean, I’ve never had anyone else react like they heard him too.\n\nBut in the moment, it’s so clear—like Garrett is standing right behind me—that I guess there’s a split second where I feel like, “How could no one else hear that?” Then I remind myself he’s dead and this is something happening to me. I’m not saying other people can hear it… I don’t think that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you thought someone else might actually be able to hear Garrett’s voice too?\n\nPatient: Not in a real, settled way, no. I don’t think I’ve ever truly believed someone else could hear him too.\n\nIt’s more that when it happens, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room—that for a second I almost expect the world to react to it. Like, “How is this only me?” But then I remember… Garrett’s dead, and I know it has to be something I’m experiencing.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that Garrett has actually returned in a way that others should be able to notice, like he’s physically back among people?\n\nPatient: No… not like that. I don’t think I’ve believed Garrett is physically back, like other people should be seeing him walking around or noticing him.\n\nIt’s more… the voice is the problem. That’s why I came in, really. I keep hearing him so clearly—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room—and even though part of me knows he’s dead, when it’s happening it feels completely real. But I’m not telling myself he’s back among people, if that distinction makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any firm beliefs or thoughts that Garrett has returned in some form—like through a spiritual presence, reincarnation, or some other way—that you feel strongly is true, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not as a firm belief.\n\nI mean, I’ve wondered things in that loose, grief kind of way—like, “Is there some part of him still around?”—but I don’t think I’ve felt strongly that he’s reincarnated or spiritually present in a way that’s definitely true. I’m trying not to make it sound more normal than it is, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had a fixed belief like that.\n\nIs that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other experiences where you see, feel, or smell something that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the hearing part. I don’t think I’ve had much with seeing or smelling things, at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nWhat’s been frightening is Garrett’s voice—hearing it as clearly as if he’s standing right behind me, like on the trail or in my painting room. And I know, rationally, that he’s dead… but in that moment it feels completely real. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even briefly, felt completely certain that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back in some way—despite knowing he’s passed away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say “completely certain” in a belief sense. I’m not trying to dodge the question.\n\nWhen it happens, though—when I hear Garrett’s voice, like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—it feels completely real in that moment. Like my body reacts as if he’s there. But another part of me knows he’s dead, so afterward I can say, “No, he hasn’t come back.”\n\nDoes that make sense? It’s not that I believe he’s returned exactly… it’s that hearing him feels that real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a short time, believed with complete certainty that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back—despite knowing he has passed away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not with complete certainty, no.\n\nI can see why you’re asking, because in the moment it can feel… very convincing, physically, like I’m reacting before I’ve had a chance to think. But I don’t believe he’s actually come back. At least, I don’t think I’ve crossed into being certain of that.\n\nI’m trying to answer that accurately and not just give the “right” answer. The closest I can say is that it can feel very real for a moment, like emotionally or physically real, but I don’t think I’ve ever been completely sure he’d actually come back. At least not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a brief moment, believed in a way that felt unshakable at the time—no matter how strange it might seem—that Garrett had somehow truly returned to you, even though you knew he was gone?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking. I do know he’s dead.\n\nBut when I hear his voice, it’s so clear—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in that moment it doesn’t feel like just a vivid feeling. It feels real. Then afterward I can tell myself it must be my mind doing something, but while it’s happening… I’m not so sure.\n\nDoctor: When you hear Garrett’s voice, do you ever think that others might be able to hear it too, or do you understand that it’s something only you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I think… I understand it’s only me hearing it. I mean, I’ve never had anyone else react like they heard him too.\n\nBut in the moment, it’s so clear—like Garrett is standing right behind me—that I guess there’s a split second where I feel like, “How could no one else hear that?” Then I remind myself he’s dead and this is something happening to me. I’m not saying other people can hear it… I don’t think that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you thought someone else might actually be able to hear Garrett’s voice too?\n\nPatient: Not in a real, settled way, no. I don’t think I’ve ever truly believed someone else could hear him too.\n\nIt’s more that when it happens, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me, on the trail or in my painting room—that for a second I almost expect the world to react to it. Like, “How is this only me?” But then I remember… Garrett’s dead, and I know it has to be something I’m experiencing.\n\nI’m trying to be accurate—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that Garrett has actually returned in a way that others should be able to notice, like he’s physically back among people?\n\nPatient: No… not like that. I don’t think I’ve believed Garrett is physically back, like other people should be seeing him walking around or noticing him.\n\nIt’s more… the voice is the problem. That’s why I came in, really. I keep hearing him so clearly—like he’s standing right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room—and even though part of me knows he’s dead, when it’s happening it feels completely real. But I’m not telling myself he’s back among people, if that distinction makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any firm beliefs or thoughts that Garrett has returned in some form—like through a spiritual presence, reincarnation, or some other way—that you feel strongly is true, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not as a firm belief.\n\nI mean, I’ve wondered things in that loose, grief kind of way—like, “Is there some part of him still around?”—but I don’t think I’ve felt strongly that he’s reincarnated or spiritually present in a way that’s definitely true. I’m trying not to make it sound more normal than it is, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had a fixed belief like that.\n\nIs that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other experiences where you see, feel, or smell something that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the hearing part. I don’t think I’ve had much with seeing or smelling things, at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nWhat’s been frightening is Garrett’s voice—hearing it as clearly as if he’s standing right behind me, like on the trail or in my painting room. And I know, rationally, that he’s dead… but in that moment it feels completely real. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even briefly, felt completely certain that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back in some way—despite knowing he’s passed away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say “completely certain” in a belief sense. I’m not trying to dodge the question.\n\nWhen it happens, though—when I hear Garrett’s voice, like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—it feels completely real in that moment. Like my body reacts as if he’s there. But another part of me knows he’s dead, so afterward I can say, “No, he hasn’t come back.”\n\nDoes that make sense? It’s not that I believe he’s returned exactly… it’s that hearing him feels that real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a short time, believed with complete certainty that Garrett was actually present with you—like he had truly come back—despite knowing he has passed away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not with complete certainty, no.\n\nI can see why you’re asking, because in the moment it can feel… very convincing, physically, like I’m reacting before I’ve had a chance to think. But I don’t believe he’s actually come back. At least, I don’t think I’ve crossed into being certain of that.\n\nI’m trying to answer that accurately and not just give the “right” answer. But my best answer is no—not complete certainty.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you were absolutely sure—without any doubt—that Garrett had come back, even if only for a few seconds?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had that absolute “no doubt” feeling.\n\nI’m sorry, I know you’re trying to get at something specific. The closest I can say is that it can feel very real for a moment, like emotionally or physically real, but I don’t think I’ve ever been completely sure he’d actually come back. At least not in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a brief moment, believed in a way that felt unshakable at the time—no matter how strange it might seem—that Garrett had somehow truly returned to you, even though you knew he was gone?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking. I don’t think I can honestly say yes to “unshakable.”\n\nIt’s more like… it can hit me hard enough that I pause, or I turn around, or I feel like he’s near me. But there’s still some part of me that knows that can’t be true. I might not feel calm about it in the moment, but I don’t think I’ve believed it with no doubt.\n\nI hope that’s answering it the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, even for a short time, that Garrett's presence was real in any way beyond just hearing his voice—like thinking he was actually with you in some physical or tangible form?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not in a physical or tangible way.\n\nI mean, sometimes I can feel affected by it, like my body reacts as if someone is there, but I don’t think I’ve believed Garrett was literally in the room or that I could touch him or anything like that. If that distinction makes sense.\n\nI’m trying not to over-explain it, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a moment, thought that Garrett was actually in the room with you in a way that you could see, feel, or interact with him, even if you knew it might not make sense?\n\nPatient: Not really in a way I could see him or touch him, no.\n\nWhat I came in about is more that I hear Garrett’s voice so clearly it’s like he’s standing right behind me—on the trail, or sometimes in my painting room. And I know, at least part of me knows, that he’s dead. But in the moment it feels completely real, like he’s right there. I don’t think I’ve actually interacted with him physically, if that’s the distinction you’re making.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you believed something about Garrett returning that you later realized wasn’t true, even if it only lasted a short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… at least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nI’ve had moments where it felt confusing or emotionally intense, and afterward I sort of had to steady myself and remind myself what’s real. But believing he had actually returned, and then later realizing that belief was false… I don’t think I’ve had that in a definite way.\n\nI hope that makes sense. I’m not trying to split hairs—I just don’t want to say yes to something stronger than what I’ve actually experienced.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever, even for a moment, believed something about Garrett returning that wasn't true, like he was actually alive again or had come back in a way that others should be able to see or hear, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no.\n\nI mean… I’ve had moments where it feels very convincing emotionally, and I can get shaken by it, but I don’t think I’ve believed he was actually alive again or that other people should be able to see or hear him.\n\nIf I’m understanding what you’re asking, I’d say no—not in that concrete way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you believed Garrett was physically present in a way that others around you should also be able to see or interact with him, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not where I thought other people should be able to see him or interact with him too.\n\nIt’s more… I can understand why you’re asking, and I’m trying to be accurate. I’ve had things feel very real to me internally, but I don’t think I’ve crossed into believing he was physically there in the room in a way someone else would confirm. Does that answer what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
